1. Parse `$ARGUMENTS` for attached command suffixes (`:auto` or `:confirm`). Canonical mode syntax is `/deep:review:auto` and `/deep:review:confirm`.
2. Treat the target text, pre-bound setup answers and every flag in the Workflow Flag Surface table below as workflow inputs, not execution modes. The table is the only list of flags.
3. If `:auto` is present, set `execution_mode = AUTONOMOUS` and resolve required setup inputs through the presentation contract's three-tier auto setup resolution before loading YAML.
4. If `:confirm` is present, set `execution_mode = INTERACTIVE` and use the presentation contract's consolidated setup prompt before loading YAML.
5. If no mode suffix is present, set `execution_mode = ASK` and use the presentation contract's consolidated setup prompt to ask for execution mode.
6. Load the selected workflow asset only after `review_target`, `review_target_type`, `review_dimensions`, `spec_folder`, `execution_mode`, `maxIterations`, `convergenceThreshold`, `convergence_mode`, `depth_mode`, `stop_policy`, `lineage_mode` and `dry_run` are bound.

### Workflow Flag Surface

The argument hint names the invocation shape; the whole flag surface is here. Every flag below
is a workflow input, never an execution mode, and the presentation asset owns how each one binds
into `config`.

| Flag | Value | Effect |
|------|-------|--------|
| `--spec-folder` | `PATH` | Binds the packet that owns this run's writes. |
| `--max-iterations` | `N` | Hard ceiling on loop iterations. |
| `--convergence` | `N` | Threshold the loop must clear before convergence may stop it. |
| `--convergence-mode` | `default` \| `off` \| `sliding-window` \| `divergent` | `default` keeps the anti-convergence `minIterations` floor. `off` disables convergence-driven STOP while max-iterations, pause and halt stay active. `sliding-window` scores novelty over a bounded window, five snapshots by default. `divergent` routes an eligible convergence stop into a Council pivot rather than ending the run. |
| `--depth-mode` | `default` \| `expansive` | `default`, the value when the flag is absent, runs exactly as before. `expansive` is opt-in: the run seeds depth-1 sub-target nodes from the review target before iteration 1 (merges are pruned as `merge-no-own-diff` and index rebuilds as `generated-artifact`), admits leaf `frontier_proposal` rows under depth, fan-out, budget, dedupe and cycle caps (`expansiveDepth` in the config: `maxDepth` 3, `maxChildrenPerNode` 6, a 50/35/15 iteration budget across depths), keeps node status in `review/frontier-nodes.jsonl`, holds open nodes as `searchDebt`, dispatches the active node's next uncovered dimension, and turns a review stop candidate into another iteration while nodes stay open. A node needs one iteration per dimension in its universe, the four canonical dimensions plus any custom name, and the depth-1 band gets only `budgetSplit.depth1` (0.5) of `--max-iterations`, so set `--max-iterations` to at least `2 * ceil(nodes / lineages) * dimensions` to fund the depth-1 band. It never outranks the iteration cap, stuck recovery, pause, `--convergence-mode=off` or `--stop-policy=max-iterations`. Fan-out lineages each get a share of the seeded nodes. |
| `--lineage-timeout-hours` | `N` | See Lineage Timeout Flag below. |
| `--stop-policy` | `convergence` \| `max-iterations` | See Stop Policy Flag below. |
| `--no-resource-map` | no value | Suppresses the resource-map write; the review report still emits. |
| `--dry-run` | no value | See Dry-Run Flag below. |
| `--review-focus` | `"TEXT"` | Optional operator focus that every iteration covers in addition to its dimension, in single-executor and fan-out runs alike. It is cut to one line of at most 1000 characters, stored as `reviewFocus` in the config and shown in the review report. |
| `--restart`, `--lineage-mode` | `auto` \| `resume` \| `restart` | Session lifecycle intent, default `auto`. `resume` keeps the same `sessionId` and archives nothing; `restart`, which bare `--restart` selects, opens a new `sessionId` at generation+1 and archives the prior `review/`. `fork` is not accepted. |
| `--extend` | positive integer, as `--extend=N` | Extends a completed run in place by `N` iterations, lineage mode `completed-continue`. The run keeps its `sessionId` and generation, records one `run_extended` ledger event and keeps the prior review report as `review/review-report-v{generation}-r{continuedFromRun}.md`. The run must be complete, so use `--lineage-mode=resume` for an unfinished one. Refused with fan-out. Under `stopPolicy: convergence` an extension with nothing new can re-stop on `all_dimensions_clean` at once: pass `--convergence-mode=off` or use `stopPolicy: max-iterations`. |
| `--extend-minutes` | positive integer, as `--extend-minutes=M` | Minutes added to the duration budget on an extension, default the prior `maxDurationMinutes`. The new budget is `max(prior, elapsed) + M`, because the clock counts idle time since `createdAt`, so an extension started after the old budget still runs. Valid only with `--extend`. |
| `--executor` | `TYPE` | Selects the executor kind, and is repeatable. Each occurrence opens a group accepting `--model=X`, `--config-dir=PATH`, `--reasoning-effort=LEVEL`, `--service-tier=TIER`, `--executor-timeout=SECONDS`, `--iters=N`, `--count=N` and `--label=X`. Which of those a given kind supports is in the presentation asset. |
| `--executors` | `JSON` | Declares the executor matrix in one value; one expanded lineage selects `config.executor`, while two or more select `config.fanout`. |
| `--concurrency` | `N` | Caps concurrent lineages in the fan-out pool. Default `2`. |

Fan-out policy: zero or one `--executor` runs the single-executor path, and so does an
`--executors` matrix that expands to exactly one lineage. Its `iterations` value binds to
`config.maxIterations`, and its executor settings populate `config.executor`. Two or more
`--executor` flags, a matrix expanding to two or more lineages, or any `--count` above 1
selects fan-out. A direct call of the fan-out runner that expands to one research or review
lineage is refused and names this workflow instead, because the runner alone skips packet
init, synthesis and save. `--fanout-lineage-artifact-dir` is internal and
set by the runtime, not by a caller. Under `:auto` a `PRE-BOUND SETUP ANSWERS:` block in the

### Workflow Flag Surface

The argument hint names the invocation shape; the whole flag surface is here. Every flag below
is a workflow input, never an execution mode, and the presentation asset owns how each one binds
into `config`.

| Flag | Value | Effect |
|------|-------|--------|
| `--spec-folder` | `PATH` | Binds the packet that owns this run's writes. |
| `--max-iterations` | `N` | Hard ceiling on loop iterations. |
| `--convergence` | `N` | Threshold the loop must clear before convergence may stop it. |
| `--convergence-mode` | `default` \| `off` \| `sliding-window` \| `divergent` | `default` keeps the anti-convergence `minIterations` floor. `off` disables convergence-driven STOP while max-iterations, pause and halt stay active. `sliding-window` scores novelty over a bounded window, five snapshots by default, on the ordinary legal-stop path. `divergent` sends a `composite_converged` or `all_questions_answered` stop into a one-round three-seat Council pivot instead of ending the run, at proportionally higher cost. |
| `--depth-mode` | `default` \| `expansive` | `default`, the value when the flag is absent, runs exactly as before. `expansive` is opt-in: the run seeds depth-1 frontier nodes from the topic before iteration 1, admits leaf `frontier_proposal` rows as new questions under depth, fan-out, budget, dedupe and cycle caps (`expansiveDepth` in the config: `maxDepth` 3, `maxChildrenPerNode` 6, a 50/35/15 iteration budget across depths), steers Next Focus to the shallowest open node, and turns a convergence stop into another iteration while frontier nodes stay open. It never outranks the iteration cap, stuck recovery, pause, `--convergence-mode=off` or `--stop-policy=max-iterations`. Fan-out lineages each get a share of the seeded nodes. |
| `--lineage-timeout-hours` | `N` | See Lineage Timeout Flag below. |
| `--stop-policy` | `convergence` \| `max-iterations` | See Stop Policy Flag below. |
| `--no-resource-map` | no value | Suppresses the `research/resource-map.md` write; `research.md` still emits. |
| `--dry-run` | no value | See Dry-Run Flag below. |
| `--restart` | no value | Shorthand that selects `restart`: opens a new `sessionId` at generation+1 and archives the prior `research/`. |
| `--lineage-mode` | `auto` \| `resume` \| `restart` | Session lifecycle intent, default `auto`. `resume` keeps the same `sessionId` and archives nothing. `restart` opens a new `sessionId` at generation+1 and archives the prior `research/`. `fork` is not accepted. |
| `--extend` | positive integer, as `--extend=N` | Extends a completed run in place by `N` iterations, lineage mode `completed-continue`. The run keeps its `sessionId` and generation, records one `run_extended` ledger event and keeps the prior synthesis as `research/synthesis-v{generation}-r{continuedFromRun}.md`. The run must be complete, so use `--lineage-mode=resume` for an unfinished one. Refused with fan-out. Under `stopPolicy: convergence` an extension with nothing new can re-stop on `all_questions_answered` at once: pass `--convergence-mode=off`, use `stopPolicy: max-iterations` or add questions to `research/inbox.jsonl`. |
| `--extend-minutes` | positive integer, as `--extend-minutes=M` | Minutes added to the duration budget on an extension, default the prior `maxDurationMinutes`. The new budget is `max(prior, elapsed) + M`, because the clock counts idle time since `createdAt`, so an extension started after the old budget still runs. Valid only with `--extend`. |
| `--executor` | `TYPE` | Selects the executor kind, and is repeatable. Each occurrence opens a group accepting `--model=X`, `--config-dir=PATH`, `--reasoning-effort=LEVEL`, `--service-tier=TIER`, `--executor-timeout=SECONDS`, `--iters=N`, `--count=N` and `--label=X`. Which of those a given kind supports, and the fail-fast on an unsupported pairing, are in the presentation asset. |
| `--executors` | `JSON` | Declares the executor matrix in one value; one expanded lineage selects `config.executor`, while two or more select `config.fanout`. |
| `--concurrency` | `N` | Caps concurrent lineages in the fan-out pool. Default `2`. |

Fan-out policy: zero or one `--executor` runs the single-executor path, and so does an

#### Step 10: Write Delta

- Create `review/deltas/iter-NNN.jsonl` once — the multi-line delta artifact the reducer consumes, separate from the one-record event file handed to the gateway in Step 9.
- Its first line MUST contain the canonical iteration record built in Step 9.
- Append structured finding, classification, traceability-check, graph-event, and ruled-out records after it, one JSON object per line.
- Never overwrite an existing delta file.

**Frontier proposals (expansive depth runs only).** When the iteration prompt carries a FRONTIER block, you may append `frontier_proposal` rows to the delta, at most the number that block allows, one per sub-target the evidence opened inside the node you were reviewing:
`{"type":"frontier_proposal","parentId":"<the node id the prompt named>","question":"<what to review, not a restatement of an existing node>","scope":"<paths inside the review target>","evidence":"<file:line that opened it>","budget":<optional positive integer, never above the parent's>}`
You only propose. The workflow's admission step decides, and it prunes a row that lacks evidence or scope, sits outside the review target, repeats a known node, or would exceed the depth, fan-out or budget caps. Never write `review/frontier-nodes.jsonl`, `searchDebt` or any coverage surface, and never emit a `frontier_proposal` row when the prompt has no FRONTIER block. When the block names a node, add `"frontierNodeId"` to the iteration record as the block says.

- Verify the narrative contains focus, finding severity sections, traceability checks, edge cases, ruled-out section, and next focus.
- Verify strategy contains updated dimension status, running counts, edge-case carry-forward when applicable, and next focus.
- Verify the append gateway returned exit 0 with a receipt, and the refreshed `review/deep-review-state.jsonl` projection shows exactly one new canonical iteration record with complete route proof.
- Verify `review/deltas/iter-NNN.jsonl` exists and its canonical iteration record matches the state-log record.
- Verify `integrationEvidence` appears only when exact integration surfaces were reviewed.

**newInfoRatio calculation**:
- Fully new findings count as 1.0.
- Partially new findings count as 0.5.
- `newInfoRatio = (fully_new + 0.5 * partially_new) / total_findings`.
- If there are no findings, set 0.0.
- Add a +0.10 simplicity bonus, capped at 1.0, when synthesis reduces open questions, resolves contradictions, or provides a cleaner model without new external evidence.

---
title: "Depth mode flag (--depth-mode)"
description: "The --depth-mode flag on /deep:research and /deep:review selects default or expansive depth, and leaves the registry-driven workflowMode, runtimeLoopType and backendKind classification unchanged."
trigger_phrases:
  - "--depth-mode"
  - "depth mode flag"
  - "expansive depth flag"
  - "deep loop depth mode"
version: 1.0.2.0
---

# Depth mode flag (--depth-mode)

## 1. OVERVIEW

`--depth-mode` is a command-level option on `/deep:research` and `/deep:review`. It takes `default` or `expansive`. The flag is not part of `mode-registry.json`, so the hub classifies a request into the same workflow mode with or without it. The behavior the flag turns on lives in the research and review packets.

---

## 2. HOW IT WORKS

### Hub Classification Unchanged

`mode-registry.json` carries the `workflowMode`, `runtimeLoopType` and `backendKind` discriminator for `research` and `review`, and it has no depth field. Routing resolves the same packet whether the flag is present or not.

### Flag Routing

The command workflow reads `--depth-mode` and binds it as `depth_mode`. The runtime acts only on the value `expansive`. `runtime/scripts/convergence.cjs` adds `depthMode` to its stop-decision config only when the value is `expansive`, and `runtime/scripts/fanout-run.cjs` passes `depthMode` to fan-out lineages only when the value is `expansive`. A default run carries no depth key in either place.

### Packet Behavior

The expansive behavior is documented in the packet catalogs: [expansive depth mode for research](../../deep-research/feature-catalog/convergence/expansive-depth-mode.md) and [expansive depth mode for review](../../deep-review/feature-catalog/loop-lifecycle/expansive-depth-mode.md).

---

| `.skilled/commands/deep/review.md` | Command doc | The `--depth-mode` flag row for review. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/scripts/convergence.cjs` | Script | Adds `depthMode` to the stop-decision config only when expansive. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/scripts/fanout-run.cjs` | Script | Passes `depthMode` to lineages only when expansive. |

## 1. DURABLE DIRECTIVE
<!-- FILL: Write one sentence: what the packet is for, not how. GOOD: Keep only the newest n archives on request. WEAK: Improve the script. -->

**Objective:** Confirm that every user-visible feature and fix of phases 001-013 has the manual testing playbook scenario its phase wrote, give each a feature catalog entry, and close the program with both alignment checks reporting no open finding.

### Decisions

Frozen choices. Changing one is an amendment.

| ID | Decision |
|----|----------|
| D1 | Each implementation phase writes its own playbook scenarios through `/create:manual-testing-playbook` (operator decision 2026-11-17); this phase only verifies coverage and routes a gap to its owning phase. |
| D2 | Catalog entries are written only through `/create:feature-catalog`; other doc findings are fixed here by direct edit, and a compiled contract they make stale is regenerated, never hand-edited. |
| D3 | This phase changes docs only; a code finding goes back to its owning phase and blocks closure until fixed or waived by the operator. |
| D4 | This phase starts only after phases 001-013 are complete. |

## 1. OVERVIEW

### Purpose

Use this quick reference when you need the command shape, artifact names, convergence rules, and synthesis outputs for the autonomous deep-review loop without loading the full protocol references.

### When to Use

- Looking up the canonical `/deep:review` invocation without re-reading the full SKILL.md.
- Confirming default thresholds, verdicts, or release-readiness states at a glance.
- Refreshing the artifact-name and packet-file checklist before a manual orchestration pass.
- Triaging a stuck loop and need the fastest path to convergence semantics.

### Commands

| Command | Description |
|---------|-------------|
| `/deep:review:auto "target"` | Run autonomous review (no approval gates) |
| `/deep:review:confirm "target"` | Run review with approval gates at each iteration |
| `/deep:review "target"` | Ask which mode to use |

### Parameters

| Parameter | Default | Description |
|-----------|---------|-------------|
| `--max-iterations` | 7 | Maximum review iterations |
| `--convergence` | 0.10 | Base sensitivity for review convergence |
| `--depth-mode` | default | `expansive` seeds depth-1 sub-target nodes, reviews the active node's next uncovered dimension, and vetoes a stop while nodes stay open |
| `--stop-policy` | convergence | Use `max-iterations` when convergence must not end the run before the iteration ceiling |
| `--spec-folder` | auto | Target spec folder path |
| `--restart` | off | Archive the current review packet and start a fresh lineage |
| `--severity-threshold` | P2 | Minimum severity to report |

---

## 2. WHEN TO USE

| Scenario | Use |
|----------|-----|
| Multi-pass code quality audit | `/deep:review` |
| Simple single-pass code review | `sk-code` (code-review mode) |
| Pre-release readiness check | `/deep:review:auto "spec folder"` |
| Spec/implementation alignment check | `/deep:review:auto "skill sk-name"` |
| Deep technical investigation | `/deep:research` (different skill) |

[0m$ [0mcd /Users/michelkerkmeester/worktrees/public/455-expansive-depth-integration && sed -n '235,275p' README.md && echo "=== review.yaml 380-420" && sed -n '380,420p' .skilled/commands/deep/assets/deep-review.yaml && echo "=== review.yaml 860-895" && sed -n '860,895p' .skilled/commands/deep/assets/deep-review.yaml
### You Just Ask

You never need to remember a command. Describe the task in plain words and the session handles the rest. On every prompt, the [skill advisor](#skill-advisor) reads the request and tells the agent which skill fits. When a workflow fits too, the brief names its command and how to run it: start it now, start it with its setup questions or offer it to you first.

The session automation covers the rest of the path. The [spec gate](#spec-gate) asks once which spec folder the work belongs to. The [session lifecycle](#session-lifecycle) hooks prime the context at start and save continuity at stop and compaction. Near the context limit, [session handover](#session-handover) carries the work into a fresh session.

The commands below are what runs underneath. Type one yourself only when you want a specific workflow or mode.

### Spec Kit Commands

`/speckit:complete` runs the whole path: intake, research, plan, build, verify and save. `/speckit:plan` stops after the plan, so someone can review it before any code is written. `/speckit:implement` builds from an existing plan. Each one accepts `:auto` to run without stopping or `:confirm` to pause at each step.

`/speckit:save` and `/speckit:resume` carry the work across sessions, as [Continuity](#continuity) describes. `/speckit:search` runs the trigger index and the ripgrep recipes over spec docs and skill docs. `/speckit:handoff` hands the work to a fresh session now, instead of waiting for the context limit.

### Deep Research And Deep Review

The deep loops run unattended and stop only when their own evidence says the work is done. `/deep:research` investigates a question one focused pass at a time. Results grow in one research document inside the spec folder. `/deep:review` audits code in passes, ranks every finding P0, P1 or P2 and never edits the code it reviews.

Each pass runs in a fresh context against state kept on disk. A script, not the agent, decides when to stop. A review stays open below its minimum iteration count, while a P0 is active or while a required check still fails, and it ends in a PASS, CONDITIONAL or FAIL verdict. A loop survives crashes and new sessions, because its progress lives in files.

Two opt-in flags let a loop reach further. `--depth-mode=expansive` keeps a frontier of sub-questions or sub-targets and turns a stop into another pass while one stays open, though never past the iteration cap. `--extend=N` reopens a finished single-executor run in the same session with room for N more passes, and `--extend-minutes=M` adds time. Under the default stop policy it can re-stop at once, so pair it with `--convergence-mode=off` when the run must keep going. A review can also track custom dimensions beside its four defaults.

A loop can also spread across several AI CLIs at once. Each executor runs its own lineage, and for a review the strictest verdict wins the merge. A pool caps how many lineages run at once. A stall watchdog stops a lineage that stops making progress, and a cost cap bounds what each one spends. The [Deep Loop README](.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/README.md) describes both modes and the shared runtime.

### CLI Orchestration

Your main CLI does not have to handle every task itself. The CLI orchestration hub hands a task to another AI CLI on the same machine and reads the result back. The agent you are talking to stays in charge. It writes the brief, waits for the answer and checks it before using it.

This lets you mix subscriptions. You can work in one CLI and send research, reviews or bulk work to another, billed to that CLI's own plan. The bridges are provider agnostic. Each CLI runs whatever providers and models you have set up in it. That is why Skilled supports eight runtimes: whichever plans you already pay for, one runtime is your main CLI and the others receive the work you hand off.

You ask for it in plain words, for example "use cli-opencode to review this diff". The advisor routes the request to the matching bridge. The bridge checks that its CLI is installed and that the dispatch passes its hard rules, then runs it. The deep loops use the same bridges, so one research or review run can spread its passes across several models. [Runtimes And CLI Bridges](#10--runtimes-and-cli-bridges) lists all eight.

### Create And Update Skills, Agents And Commands

Extending Skilled follows the same framework that built it. The [sk-doc](.skilled/skills/sk-doc/README.md) hub holds a template, a set of standards and a validator for each kind of component. Ask for a new skill, agent or command in plain words and the advisor loads the matching sk-doc mode, so the result matches the rest of the repository.

Each kind also has a `/create` command for when you want the workflow directly. `/create:sk-skill` creates or updates a skill, and `/create:skill-parent` scaffolds a parent hub with its modes. `/create:agent` and `/create:command` cover agents and slash commands. `/create:readme`, `/create:changelog`, `/create:goal` and `/create:repo-rule` cover the documents around them. Every one runs with `:auto` or with `:confirm` to pause at each step.

Updating works the same way as creating. Name the component and the change, and the workflow edits it in place, checks it against its template and runs the sk-doc validator. A new skill needs no registration step, because the advisor finds it from its own metadata.

[0m$ [0mcd /Users/michelkerkmeester/worktrees/public/455-expansive-depth-integration && sed -n '55,100p' .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/references/convergence/convergence.md
### Convergence Modes

The shared convergence runtime accepts four values. The workflow keeps hard terminal boundaries outside mode-specific convergence handling.

| Mode | Live Behavior |
|------|---------------|
| `default` | Uses the normal full-history convergence signals and sends a legal STOP to synthesis. This is the fallback when no mode is supplied. |
| `off` | Skips convergence-driven STOP candidates. Max iterations, pause or cancellation, manual stop, recovery halt, and unrecoverable error behavior remain active. |
| `sliding-window` | Uses a bounded graph-novelty window, defaulting to 5 snapshots, while preserving the ordinary legal-stop and synthesis path. |
| `divergent` | Computes convergence normally, but translates an eligible legal STOP into a three-seat direction-selection pivot and continues from the selected focus. |

#### Expansive Depth Mode

`--depth-mode=expansive` grows a frontier of follow-up questions beside the questions seeded at init. The value lands in `antiConvergence.depthMode`, and the caps live in `expansiveDepth`: `maxDepth` 3, `maxChildrenPerNode` 6, `budgetSplit` 0.5/0.35/0.15 for depths 1, 2 and 3, and `maxFrontierNodes`, which defaults to half of `maxIterations` unless the config sets a number. The run cap never prunes a depth-1 seed, which only the root-children cap bounds, but every seed that is not pruned, answered ones included, counts toward the live total that later admissions are checked against. A fan-out parent seeds before any config exists, so its cap falls back to 10. An absent flag means `default`, which keeps the default behavior; `research-depth-mode-golden.vitest` proves it after stripping the expansive-only persona paragraph from the CLI lineage prompt, the one byte change.

After the `minIterations` floor, an expansive run with open or active frontier nodes turns a soft stop candidate into CONTINUE with reasonDetail `frontierOpen`. The veto never creates a stop of its own and never outranks the iteration cap, consecutive failures, stuck recovery, `--convergence-mode=off` or `--stop-policy=max-iterations`. Under `--convergence-mode=divergent` the pivot becomes eligible only once no frontier node is open or active.

Tested in `stop-decision.vitest`.

#### Divergent Pivot Contract

Research pivots are eligible only when the workflow decision is `STOP` and the exact internal reason is `composite_converged` or `all_questions_answered`. The legal-stop and quality gates run before this branch. `maxIterationsReached`, `blockedStop`, `stuckRecovery`, `minIterationsNotReached`, `error`, `manualStop`, and `userPaused` are explicitly excluded. Max iterations, pause or cancellation, manual stop, and unrecoverable error therefore never dispatch a pivot Council.

Each eligible pivot runs one native, in-process Council round with exactly three distinct seats: analytical, critical, and pragmatic.
If that round misses quorum, because a seat fails to return or returns a report that does not parse, the pivot reopens once as attempt 2 with three fresh seat outputs. A second quorum miss, or three valid seats that do not agree, fails the pivot closed. `maxCouncilSeatOutputs` caps one council attempt at 3 seat outputs, so a pivot records at most 6.

- It does not use an external CLI.
- The transaction requires all three seat returns to be parse-valid and at least two seats to materially endorse the same candidate without a high-severity blocker.
- Research candidates come from persisted adjacent questions, contradiction or verification gaps, missing source classes, alternate evidence methods, and independent checks of recent findings.
- A completed pivot restores the selected candidate's focus and returns to the research loop; a failed pivot fails closed to synthesis.

Every pivot dispatches three seats, or six when its first attempt misses quorum, so a run that pivots has proportionally higher model cost and duration than the same run under `default`, `off`, or `sliding-window`. Pivot evidence is isolated under `<artifactRoot>/divergent/pivots/<pivotId>/council/**`, including config, append-only state, three seat artifacts, deliberation, and report, plus an attempt-2 folder with its own seat artifacts and deliberation after a retry. This path is distinct from ordinary planning Council artifacts under a spec packet's `ai-council/**` tree.

---

## 2. STOP CONTRACT

### stopReason Values

| Value | When It Is Used |
|-------|-----------------|
| `converged` | Legal-stop gates passed and the loop may exit normally |
| `maxIterationsReached` | The loop hit the configured iteration cap |
| `blockedStop` | A STOP candidate was blocked by legal-stop or graph gates |
| `manualStop` | The operator asked the run to stop |
| `error` | The loop hit an unrecoverable workflow or state error |

The value lands in `antiConvergence.depthMode`, and the caps live in `expansiveDepth`: `maxDepth` 3, `maxChildrenPerNode` 6, a `budgetSplit` of 0.5/0.35/0.15 across the depth bands, and `maxFrontierNodes`, which defaults to half of `maxIterations` unless the config sets a number. The run cap never prunes a depth-1 seed, which only the root-children cap bounds, but every seed that is not pruned, answered ones included, counts toward the live total that later admissions are checked against. A fan-out parent seeds before any config exists, so its cap falls back to 10. The mode is locked against the optimizer; the `expansiveDepth` block is in neither its tunable nor its locked list. An absent flag means `default`, which runs exactly as before.
107:After the review floor, an expansive run that still holds an open or active node turns a stop candidate into `CONTINUE` with reasonDetail `frontierOpen`, whichever candidate it was, `all_dimensions_clean` or a composite convergence vote. The veto never creates a stop of its own and never outranks the iteration cap, consecutive failures, stuck recovery, pause, `--convergence-mode=off`, `--stop-policy=max-iterations` or the `minIterations` floor. Under stuck recovery the handler closes the active node as `exhausted` with reason `stuck`, so a node the run cannot fund stops holding the veto open.
109:When the frontier holds at least one answered node and none is open or active, and neither `all_dimensions_clean` nor the composite vote forms, an expansive run gets the hard candidate `frontier_drained`. It ranks after the other two, so the floor, the legal-stop gates and the graph decision run on it as on any candidate: a failed gate gives `BLOCKED`, and a stop records `converged` with reasonDetail `frontier_drained`. A fan-out lineage reads only its own partition, so one whose nodes are answered stops rather than running to its cap. A frontier that closed with nothing answered, every node exhausted or pruned, forms no candidate, and a default run never forms it.
183:6. **Stop candidate** -- all dimensions covered and clean (every configured dimension covered, the four by default, no active P0/P1, coverage stabilized) is a hard candidate; otherwise the 3-signal composite vote with threshold 0.60 is a soft one. Under `--depth-mode=expansive`, a frontier with at least one answered node and none open or active is a third, hard candidate (`frontier_drained`) when neither forms. No candidate returns `CONTINUE` (`noStopCandidate`).
185:8. **Frontier veto** -- under `--depth-mode=expansive`, an open frontier node turns the candidate into `CONTINUE` (`frontierOpen`).
611:- Depth mode (`antiConvergence.depthMode`); the `expansiveDepth` caps are in neither the tunable nor the locked list

// The frontier node store is append-only and the last row per node wins, so a
// reduce rebuilds the same node list every time it reads the same file. The
// history keeps every valid row in file order: a status a node has since left
// still has to attribute the iterations it spent there.
function readReviewFrontierStore(storePath) {
  if (!fs.existsSync(storePath)) return { nodes: [], history: [] };
  const byId = new Map();
  const history = [];
  for (const line of readUtf8(storePath).split('\n')) {
    if (!line.trim()) continue;
    let row;
    try { row = JSON.parse(line); } catch { continue; }
    if (!row || typeof row !== 'object' || typeof row.nodeId !== 'string' || !row.nodeId) continue;
    byId.set(row.nodeId, row);
    history.push(row);
  }
  return {
    nodes: [...byId.values()].sort((left, right) => left.nodeId.localeCompare(right.nodeId)),
    history,
  };
}

// A focus carries the node it was dispatched to as a marker phrase, and a bare
// substring match would let a node claim a longer id that starts with its own,
// so the id is bounded on both sides.
function frontierMarkerPattern(nodeId) {
  return new RegExp(`(?:^|[^\\w-])Frontier node ${escapeRegExp(String(nodeId))}(?![\\w-])`);
}

// A leaf can reword a focus before it reaches the state log, so the marker alone
// cannot attribute every visit. A store row that marked the node active on an
// iteration's number claims that iteration's record either way.
function reviewRecordsForNode(nodeId, history, iterationRecords) {
  const marker = frontierMarkerPattern(nodeId);
  const activeIterations = new Set(
    (Array.isArray(history) ? history : [])
      .filter((row) => row && row.nodeId === nodeId && row.status === 'active')
      .map((row) => getIterationRun(row))
      .filter((iteration) => iteration !== null),
  );
  return (Array.isArray(iterationRecords) ? iterationRecords : []).filter((record) => {
    if (!record || record.type !== 'iteration') return false;
    if (record.frontierNodeId === nodeId) return true;
    if (typeof record.focus === 'string' && marker.test(record.focus)) return true;
    const iteration = getIterationRun(record);
    return iteration !== null && activeIterations.has(iteration);
  });
}

// Only an attempt that produced evidence counts as a visit: an errored or timed
// out record is retried work, not depth the node has already spent.
function reviewFrontierVisits(nodeId, history, iterationRecords) {
  return reviewRecordsForNode(nodeId, history, iterationRecords)
    .filter((record) => !FRONTIER_FAILED_STATUSES.has(String(record.status || '').toLowerCase()))
    .length;
}

// Depth bands share maxIterations 50/35/15 by default (depths 0-1, 2, 3). A band's
// unspent share rolls down to the next band only once the band has no open node,
// and never up. When a band's spend reaches its share, its open nodes close as
// exhausted with reason budget, so the run stops waiting on work it cannot fund.
function applyReviewFrontierBudget(nodes, history, iterationRecords, maxIterations, budgetSplit) {
  const split = budgetSplit && typeof budgetSplit === 'object' ? budgetSplit : {};
  const shares = [
    Number.isFinite(split.depth1) ? split.depth1 : 0.5,
    Number.isFinite(split.depth2) ? split.depth2 : 0.35,
    Number.isFinite(split.depth3) ? split.depth3 : 0.15,
  ];
  const cap = Number.isFinite(maxIterations) && maxIterations > 0 ? maxIterations : 10;
  const bandOf = (depth) => (depth <= 1 ? 0 : depth === 2 ? 1 : 2);
  const spent = [0, 0, 0];
  for (const node of nodes) {
    spent[bandOf(node.depth)] += reviewFrontierVisits(node.nodeId, history, iterationRecords);
  }
  let result = nodes.map((node) => ({ ...node }));
  const budget = {};
  let carry = 0;
  for (let band = 0; band < 3; band += 1) {
    const allotted = cap * shares[band] + carry;
    if (spent[band] >= allotted) {
      result = result.map((node) => (bandOf(node.depth) === band && (node.status === 'open' || node.status === 'active')
        ? { ...node, status: 'exhausted', reason: 'budget' }
        : node));
    }
    const bandOpen = result.some((node) => bandOf(node.depth) === band && (node.status === 'open' || node.status === 'active'));
    carry = bandOpen ? 0 : Math.max(0, allotted - spent[band]);
    budget[`depth${band + 1}`] = { allotted: Math.round(allotted * 100) / 100, spent: spent[band] };
  }
  return { nodes: result, budget };
}

// A node is answered once every dimension in the review's universe has evidence
// behind it. That status is derived on every reduce instead of written back, so
// the store keeps only the lifecycle its writer owns and the projection stays a
// pure function of the rows it read.
function buildReviewFrontier(storeData, iterationRecords, config) {
  const universe = buildDimensionUniverse(config);
  const history = Array.isArray(storeData?.history) ? storeData.history : [];
  const storedNodes = Array.isArray(storeData?.nodes) ? storeData.nodes : [];
  const nodes = storedNodes.map((node) => {
    const nodeId = node.nodeId;
    const records = reviewRecordsForNode(nodeId, history, iterationRecords)
      .filter((record) => !FRONTIER_FAILED_STATUSES.has(String(record.status || '').toLowerCase()));
    const covered = new Set();
    for (const record of records) {
      if (!Array.isArray(record.dimensions)) continue;
      for (const dimension of record.dimensions) {
        const name = String(dimension).trim().toLowerCase();
        if (name) covered.add(name);
      }
    }
    const coveredDimensions = universe.filter((dimension) => covered.has(dimension));
    const stillOpen = node.status === 'open' || node.status === 'active';
    const status = stillOpen && coveredDimensions.length === universe.length ? 'answered' : node.status;
    return {
      nodeId,
      parentId: node.parentId ?? null,
      depth: node.depth,
      scope: node.scope ?? null,
      question: node.question ?? null,
      status,
      reason: status === node.status ? (node.reason ?? null) : null,
      iteration: node.iteration ?? null,
      coveredDimensions,
      nextDimension: universe.find((dimension) => !covered.has(dimension)) ?? null,
      visits: records.length,
    };
  });
  const budgeted = applyReviewFrontierBudget(
    nodes,
    history,
    iterationRecords,
    Number(config?.maxIterations),
    config?.expansiveDepth?.budgetSplit,
  );
  const projected = budgeted.nodes;
  const stats = { total: projected.length };
  for (const status of FRONTIER_STATUSES) stats[status] = projected.filter((node) => node.status === status).length;
  // Breadth first: the shallowest node, then one not yet visited, then the oldest.
  const chosen = projected
    .filter((node) => node.status === 'open' || node.status === 'active')
    .sort((left, right) => ((left.depth ?? 0) - (right.depth ?? 0))
      || (left.visits - right.visits)
      || ((left.iteration ?? 0) - (right.iteration ?? 0))
      || left.nodeId.localeCompare(right.nodeId))[0] ?? null;
  return {
    nodes: projected,
    stats,
    budget: budgeted.budget,

2. **Sanitize**: Exclude `.env`, credentials, large binaries from staging
3. **Commit**: `git commit -m "chore(deep-research): iteration {NNN} complete" -m "{one-line summary of what the iteration found}"`
4. **On commit failure**: Log warning and continue (checkpoint is non-blocking)

Checkpoint commits provide rollback points: `git log -- research/` shows the last good state for the research packet. If state corruption occurs, `git checkout HEAD~1 -- research/deep-research-state.jsonl` restores the previous version.

#### Step 5: Loop Decision
- If convergence check returns STOP: exit to synthesis
- If STUCK_RECOVERY: modify focus directive, reset stuck count, continue
- Otherwise: increment iteration counter, go to Step 1

### Expansive Depth Frontier (opt-in)

Under `--depth-mode=expansive` the loop keeps a frontier of follow-up questions beside the key questions seeded at init. The flag writes `antiConvergence.depthMode: "expansive"` and the caps in `expansiveDepth`: `maxDepth` 3, `maxChildrenPerNode` 6, a `budgetSplit` of 0.5/0.35/0.15 per depth, and `maxFrontierNodes`, which defaults to half of `maxIterations` unless the config sets a number. A run without the flag keeps the default path and its behavior. The one byte change is the expansive-only persona paragraph in the CLI lineage prompt, which the golden test strips before it compares.

`step_frontier_seed` decomposes the topic into 3 to 6 depth-1 nodes before iteration 1. On a plain run it sits in `phase_init` after `step_create_config`; on a fan-out run it runs before `step_fanout_spawn`. It calls `runtime/scripts/frontier-admit.cjs seed`.

A leaf may append `frontier_proposal` rows to its delta only when its prompt carries a FRONTIER block, which `frontier-admit.cjs block` computes from the node's remaining child slots and the prompt pack renders through `{frontier_block}`. `step_frontier_admit` runs before `step_reduce_state` and calls `frontier-admit.cjs admit`, which applies six checks in order: relevance, dedupe, depth, fan-out, budget, cycle. The first failure prunes the proposal with that reason. Admitted nodes become `research/inbox.jsonl` rows with `origin: "frontier"`.

Next Focus picks the shallowest open node first, then the least visited, then the oldest, and writes `Frontier node <id> (depth N, scope S): <question>` so a visit does not read as an answer.

Under stuck recovery the handler closes the focused node as `exhausted` with reason `stuck` through `frontier-admit.cjs close`, keeps the `stuckRecovery` event, and moves to the next open node. Global recovery runs only when no node is open.

On a fan-out run, `fanout-run.cjs --depth-mode expansive` deals the seeded open depth-1 nodes round-robin in node-id order across lineages, writes each lineage's `frontier-nodes.jsonl` and inbox rows before it spawns, binds `frontier_scope:` in the lineage prompt and records `frontier_partition` in `orchestration-summary.json`. `fanout-merge.cjs` merges the lineage frontiers, each node keeping its lineage label, and records a partition guard that names any dealt node no lineage reports.

### Ideas Backlog Convention

The canonical ideas backlog is reducer-owned and derived from JSONL lifecycle events. A persistent ideas file at `research/research-ideas.md` may still exist as operator-authored context, but leaf agents record new machine-discovered tangents with `idea_observed` events.

#### Check Points

The orchestrator checks the ideas backlog at three points:

459:Under `--depth-mode=expansive` the loop keeps a frontier of sub-target nodes beside the review target. The flag writes `antiConvergence.depthMode: "expansive"` and the caps in `expansiveDepth`: `maxDepth` 3, `maxChildrenPerNode` 6, a `budgetSplit` of 0.5/0.35/0.15 across the depth bands, and `maxFrontierNodes`, which defaults to half of `maxIterations` unless the config sets a number. A run without the flag keeps the default path and runs exactly as before, and the mode is locked against the optimizer; the `expansiveDepth` caps are in neither its tunable nor its locked list.
461:`step_frontier_seed` decomposes the review target into depth-1 sub-target nodes before iteration 1, through `runtime/scripts/frontier-admit.cjs seed --target review`. On a plain run it sits in `phase_init` after `step_create_config`; on a fan-out run it runs before `step_fanout_spawn`. A merge that carries no diff of its own is pruned `merge-no-own-diff` and an index rebuild `generated-artifact`, because neither is reviewable work.
463:`step_read_state` reads `active_frontier_node` from `registry.frontier.activeNodeId` and sets `next_dimension` to that node's next uncovered dimension, so the iteration reviews depth the node has not spent yet. `step_frontier_activate` marks the node `active` before the dispatch, and the prompt pack's `{frontier_block}` names the node; a leaf proposes `frontier_proposal` rows only when its block is non-empty.
465:`step_frontier_admit` runs before the reducer and applies six checks in order: relevance, dedupe, depth, fan-out, budget, cycle. Relevance includes a node's scope sitting inside a file-list review target. The first failure prunes the proposal under that check's name. A review run has no inbox, so the store alone queues an admitted node, and `runtime/scripts/frontier-admit.cjs --target review` is its only writer.
467:Next Focus reads, in precedence order: blocked-stop recovery, the pivot, the active frontier node, the leaf's `nextFocus`, then the first uncovered dimension. The `frontierOpen` veto holds the loop at CONTINUE while a node stays open, and never outranks the iteration cap, consecutive failures, stuck recovery, pause, `--convergence-mode=off`, `--stop-policy=max-iterations` or the review floor. Stuck recovery closes the active node `exhausted` with reason `stuck` before recovery continues. Once no node is open or active and at least one is answered, a run with no other candidate gets the hard `frontier_drained` candidate, which the legal-stop gates and the graph decision still decide.
469:On a fan-out run the depth-1 nodes are dealt round-robin in node-id order, `frontier_scope:` binds each lineage's node ids beside `review_focus:` in its prompt, and the partition is recorded as `frontier_partition` in `orchestration-summary.json`. Each lineage's store is written before it spawns, the merge returns a merged `frontier`, and a partition guard names any dealt node no lineage reports. A node needs one iteration per configured dimension, so 50% of each lineage's `maxIterations` should reach `ceil(nodes / lineages) * dimensions`; below that the depth-1 band cannot fund the nodes it was dealt.

[0m$ [0mcd /Users/michelkerkmeester/worktrees/public/455-expansive-depth-integration && sed -n '2395,2470p' .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/scripts/reduce-state.cjs
// A free-text leaf focus must not hide a required dimension the run has not covered yet.
function pickReviewBaseFocus(iterationFiles, registry) {
  const leafFocus = iterationFiles.map((iteration) => iteration.nextFocus).filter(Boolean).at(-1) || '';
  const uncovered = Object.keys(registry.dimensionCoverage).filter((dimension) => !registry.dimensionCoverage[dimension]);
  const leafKey = String(leafFocus).toLowerCase().trim();
  if (leafFocus && (uncovered.length === 0 || uncovered.some((dimension) => leafKey.includes(dimension)))) {
    return leafFocus;
  }
  return uncovered[0] || leafFocus || '[All dimensions covered]';
}

// The expansive frontier is the run's own queue, and the node it chose carries
// the dimension that node still owes, so a leaf's free-text focus cannot point
// the next iteration at work the frontier has already closed or reordered.
function pickReviewFrontierFocus(frontier) {
  if (!frontier || typeof frontier !== 'object' || !frontier.activeNodeId) return '';
  const nodes = Array.isArray(frontier.nodes) ? frontier.nodes : [];
  const node = nodes.find((candidate) => candidate && candidate.nodeId === frontier.activeNodeId);
  if (!node) return '';
  const scope = Array.isArray(node.scope) ? node.scope.join(', ') : (node.scope || 'unscoped');
  return `Frontier node ${node.nodeId} (depth ${node.depth}, scope ${scope}): ${node.question} - review the ${frontier.activeNextDimension} dimension`;
}

function updateStrategyContent(strategyContent, registry, iterationFiles, options = {}, iterationRecords = []) {
  // Early return when there is no strategy file to update. Empty content
  // cannot contain the machine-owned anchors and replaceAnchorSection would
  // correctly fail-close — but for that specific case we intentionally skip
  // rather than throw so bootstrap flows (no strategy yet) still work.
  if (!strategyContent) {
    return strategyContent;
  }

  const anchorOptions = { createMissing: Boolean(options.createMissingAnchors) };
  const severity = registry.findingsBySeverity;
  const runningFindings = [
    `- P0 (Blockers): ${severity.P0}`,
    `- P1 (Required): ${severity.P1}`,
    `- P2 (Suggestions): ${severity.P2}`,
    `- Resolved: ${registry.resolvedFindingsCount}`,
  ].join('\n');

  const completedDimensions = Object.keys(registry.dimensionCoverage)
    .filter((dimension) => registry.dimensionCoverage[dimension])
    .map((dimension) => `- [x] ${dimension}`)
    .join('\n') || '[None yet]';

  const remainingDimensions = Object.keys(registry.dimensionCoverage)
    .filter((dimension) => !registry.dimensionCoverage[dimension])
    .map((dimension) => `- [ ] ${dimension}`)
    .join('\n') || '[All dimensions complete]';

  // Default next-focus: a leaf focus naming an uncovered dimension, else the first uncovered dimension, else the leaf focus.
  let nextFocus = pickReviewBaseFocus(iterationFiles, registry);
  // An open node outranks the leaf's own focus; the pivot override and the
  // blocked-stop recovery below still outrank the node, because a saturated
  // direction or a hard block is what the run must heed before spending depth.
  const frontierFocus = pickReviewFrontierFocus(registry.frontier);
  if (frontierFocus) nextFocus = frontierFocus;
  if (registry.divergence.currentFocus) {
    nextFocus = registry.divergence.currentFocus;
  }

  // Prefer the latest blocked-stop
  // recovery only when blocked-stop is genuinely the most recent loop event.
  // The earlier implementation read timestamps from the markdown iteration
  // files, but parseIterationFile() never captures timestamps, so
  // latestIterationTimestamp was always undefined and any historical
  // blocked-stop permanently pinned next-focus. Fix: source recency from the
  // JSONL iteration records (which do carry timestamps) instead.
  const latestBlockedStop = registry.blockedStopHistory?.at(-1);
  if (latestBlockedStop && latestBlockedStop.timestamp) {
    const latestIterationTimestamp = iterationRecords
      .filter((record) => record?.type === 'iteration' && typeof record.timestamp === 'string')
      .map((record) => record.timestamp)
      .at(-1);

  if (options.triggerReason !== 'all_dimensions_clean') {
    throw new TypeError('Review divergent pivots require the all_dimensions_clean legal-stop reason.');
  }

  const config = readJson(options.configPath);
  const registry = readJson(options.registryPath);
  const strategy = readFileSync(options.strategyPath, 'utf8');
  const stateRecords = readJsonl(options.stateLogPath);
  const pivotEvents = readPivotEvents(options.artifactRoot);
  const sessionId = normalizeText(config.sessionId);
  const generation = typeof config.generation === 'number' || typeof config.generation === 'string'
    ? config.generation
    : 1;
  if (!sessionId) throw new TypeError('Review config sessionId is required for a pivot.');
  if (!normalizeText(config.reviewTarget)) throw new TypeError('Review config reviewTarget is required for boundary validation.');

  // The stop veto already keeps the run going while frontier nodes are open, so a
  // pivot reaching this point with one still open means the caller skipped it. An
  // accepted direction re-enters breadth through frontier admission as a frontier
  // proposal, which is why the pivot itself needs a closed queue.
  const frontierOpen = expansiveFrontierNodes(config, registry)
    .some((node) => ['open', 'active'].includes(normalizeText(node.status)));
  if (frontierOpen) {
    throw new TypeError('A review pivot under the expansive depth mode needs a terminal frontier; open frontier nodes remain.');
  }
  const latestIteration = stateRecords.filter((record) => record.type === 'iteration').at(-1);
  const strategyFocus = extractSection(strategy, ['12. NEXT FOCUS', '11. NEXT FOCUS']);
  const previousFocus = normalizeText(strategyFocus) || normalizeText(latestIteration?.focus);
  if (!previousFocus) throw new TypeError('A current review dimension or direction is required for a pivot.');

  const seeds = buildCandidateSeeds(config, registry, stateRecords);
  const boundaryRejections = seeds.filter(seedViolatesReadOnlyBoundary);
  const candidates = uniqueCandidates(seeds.filter((seed) => !seedViolatesReadOnlyBoundary(seed)).map(candidateFromSeed));
  const completedEvents = pivotEvents.filter((event) => event.event === 'pivot_completed');
  const selectedEvents = pivotEvents.filter((event) => event.event === 'pivot_selected');
  const rejectedEvents = pivotEvents.filter((event) => event.event === 'pivot_candidate_rejected');
  const mainOverrides = stateRecords.filter((event) => event.event === 'pivot_override_accepted');
  const saturatedDirections = [...new Set([
    ...completedEvents.flatMap((event) => readStringList(event.saturatedDirections)),
    ...completedEvents.map((event) => normalizeText(event.previousFocus)).filter(Boolean),
    ...mainOverrides.flatMap((event) => readStringList(event.saturatedDirections)),
    previousFocus,
  ])];

  const priorCandidates: PivotCandidate[] = [];
  const addPrior = (value: unknown, source: string, evidenceRef: string): void => {
    const candidate = priorCandidate(normalizeText(value), source, evidenceRef);
    if (candidate) priorCandidates.push(candidate);
  };
  addPrior(previousFocus, 'current-focus', 'deep-review-strategy.md#next-focus');
  saturatedDirections.forEach((direction, index) => addPrior(direction, 'swept-direction', `divergent/pivots#swept-${index + 1}`));
  (Array.isArray(registry.ruledOutCandidates) ? registry.ruledOutCandidates : []).filter(isRecord)
    .forEach((entry, index) => addPrior(entry.rationale || entry.reason || entry.bugClass, 'ruled-out-direction', `deep-review-findings-registry.json#ruled-out-${index + 1}`));
  (Array.isArray(registry.cleanSearchProof) ? registry.cleanSearchProof : []).filter(isRecord)
    .forEach((entry, index) => addPrior(entry.proof || entry.rationale || entry.bugClass, 'clean-search-proof', `deep-review-findings-registry.json#clean-proof-${index + 1}`));
  selectedEvents.forEach((event, index) => {
    if (isRecord(event.selectedCandidate)) priorCandidates.push(event.selectedCandidate as unknown as PivotCandidate);
    else addPrior(event.selectedCandidate, 'selected-direction', `divergent/pivots#selected-${index + 1}`);
  });
  mainOverrides.forEach((event, index) => {
    if (isRecord(event.selectedCandidate)) priorCandidates.push(event.selectedCandidate as unknown as PivotCandidate);

#### Description

A completed deep-review run extends in place by N iterations under --extend=N, keeps its session identifier and generation, and keeps the prior review report as a snapshot before re-synthesis.

#### How It Works

The run continues under lineage mode `completed-continue`, keeps the prior review report as a snapshot before re-synthesis and takes `--extend-minutes=M` on the duration budget. Extension is refused with fan-out.

#### Source Files

See [`loop-lifecycle/completed-run-extension.md`](loop-lifecycle/completed-run-extension.md) for full implementation and validation file listings.

---

### Review minIterations floor

#### Description

A review run with a configured minIterations floor returns CONTINUE with minIterationsNotReached until its iteration count reaches the floor, so a review never stops before the floor.

#### How It Works

`convergence.cjs` returns `CONTINUE` with `minIterationsNotReached` below the floor, and the command's fixed stop order checks the floor before the gates. The shipped default is 2.

#### Source Files

See [`loop-lifecycle/min-iterations-floor.md`](loop-lifecycle/min-iterations-floor.md) for full implementation and validation file listings.

---

.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/README.md:158:`--depth-mode=expansive` is the one stop-path change that grows work instead of ending it. A seed step writes the depth-1 sub-target nodes before iteration 1, the active node's next uncovered dimension steers the iteration, and an admission step before the reducer applies six checks in order, relevance, dedupe, depth, fan-out, budget and cycle, pruning the proposal on the first failure. Node status lives in `review/frontier-nodes.jsonl`, which no default run creates, and an open node is filed as open search debt, so the run spends the iteration that node still needs. The frontier veto never outranks the iteration cap, consecutive failures, stuck recovery, pause, `--convergence-mode=off`, `--stop-policy=max-iterations` or the floor above, and stuck recovery closes the active node as `exhausted`. Once every node is closed and at least one was answered, a run that forms no other stop candidate gets `frontier_drained`, which the legal-stop gates still decide, so a run whose frontier work is done stops before its cap. The caps are `maxDepth` 3, `maxChildrenPerNode` 6, a `0.5/0.35/0.15` budget split and `maxFrontierNodes`.

function normalizeDepthMode(raw) {
  if (raw === undefined || raw === false || raw === null || raw === '' || raw === 'default') return null;
  if (raw !== 'expansive') throw inputError('depthMode must be one of: default, expansive');
  return raw;
}

// Open depth-1 nodes are dealt round-robin in node-id order, so the same seed
// always gives each lineage the same share and the merge guard can check it.
function partitionFrontierNodes(baseArtifactDir, labels) {
  const { readNodeStore } = require('./frontier-admit.cjs');
  const nodes = [...readNodeStore(path.join(baseArtifactDir, 'frontier-nodes.jsonl')).values()]
    .filter((node) => node.depth === 1 && (node.status === 'open' || node.status === 'active'))
    .sort((left, right) => left.nodeId.localeCompare(right.nodeId));
  const partition = Object.fromEntries(labels.map((label) => [label, []]));
  nodes.forEach((node, index) => { partition[labels[index % labels.length]].push(node); });
  return partition;
}

// A lineage starts from its own store and inbox. They are written only when the
// lineage has no store yet, so a resumed lineage keeps the nodes it already closed.
// Review carries no inbox: its nodes reach the leaf through the FRONTIER block, so
// a seeded inbox would only hand the finding pipeline a file nothing reads.
function writeLineageFrontierStore(lineageDir, nodes, options = {}) {
  const storePath = path.join(lineageDir, 'frontier-nodes.jsonl');
  if (fs.existsSync(storePath) || nodes.length === 0) return false;
  fs.mkdirSync(lineageDir, { recursive: true });
  fs.writeFileSync(storePath, nodes.map((node) => `${JSON.stringify(node)}\n`).join(''), 'utf8');
  if (options.inbox === false) return true;
  const inboxRows = nodes.map((node) => JSON.stringify({ id: node.nodeId, text: node.question, source: 'frontier', origin: 'frontier', injectedAtIteration: 0 }));
  fs.appendFileSync(path.join(lineageDir, 'inbox.jsonl'), `${inboxRows.join('\n')}\n`, 'utf8');
  return true;
}

[0m$ [0mcd /Users/michelkerkmeester/worktrees/public/455-expansive-depth-integration && sed -n '760,810p' .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/scripts/fanout-merge.cjs
// Lineages number the nodes they admit from their own store, so two lineages
// that each hold one dealt node both name their first child F-2-002. A merged
// node id is therefore qualified by its lineage label, its parent link with it,
// and the lineage's own id stays beside it. The partition guard reads the depth-1
// split the fan-out runner recorded against those lineage ids: a node dealt to a
// lineage that no lineage reports was lost, and the guard names it instead of
// letting the merged frontier look complete.
function qualifyFrontierId(label, nodeId) {
  return typeof nodeId === 'string' && nodeId !== '' && nodeId !== 'root' ? `${label}:${nodeId}` : nodeId;
}

function mergeResearchFrontier(lineageData, frontierPartition) {
  const nodes = [];
  for (const { label, registry } of [...lineageData].sort((left, right) => compareText(left.label, right.label))) {
    const lineageNodes = Array.isArray(registry?.frontier?.nodes) ? registry.frontier.nodes : [];
    for (const node of lineageNodes) {
      nodes.push({
        ...node,
        nodeId: qualifyFrontierId(label, node.nodeId),
        lineageNodeId: node.nodeId,
        ...(Object.prototype.hasOwnProperty.call(node, 'parentId') ? { parentId: qualifyFrontierId(label, node.parentId) } : {}),
        lineage: label,
      });
    }
  }
  nodes.sort((left, right) => compareText(left.lineage, right.lineage) || compareText(left.lineageNodeId, right.lineageNodeId));
  const stats = { total: nodes.length };
  for (const status of FRONTIER_STATUSES) stats[status] = nodes.filter((node) => node.status === status).length;
  const reported = new Set(nodes.map((node) => node.lineageNodeId));
  const expected = frontierPartition && typeof frontierPartition === 'object'
    ? Object.values(frontierPartition).flatMap((ids) => (Array.isArray(ids) ? ids : []))
    : [];
  const missing = [...new Set(expected.filter((id) => !reported.has(id)))].sort(compareText);
  return {
    nodes,
    stats,
    ...(expected.length > 0 ? { partitionGuard: { passed: missing.length === 0, missing } } : {}),
  };
}

      canonicalKey: 'keyFindings',
      aliases: { findings: 'keyFindings' },
      lineage: label,
    });

.skilled/commands/deep/review.md:100:| `--depth-mode` | `default` \| `expansive` | `default`, the value when the flag is absent, runs exactly as before. `expansive` is opt-in: the run seeds depth-1 sub-target nodes from the review target before iteration 1 (merges are pruned as `merge-no-own-diff` and index rebuilds as `generated-artifact`), admits leaf `frontier_proposal` rows under depth, fan-out, budget, dedupe and cycle caps (`expansiveDepth` in the config: `maxDepth` 3, `maxChildrenPerNode` 6, a 50/35/15 iteration budget across depths), keeps node status in `review/frontier-nodes.jsonl`, holds open nodes as `searchDebt`, dispatches the active node's next uncovered dimension, and turns a review stop candidate into another iteration while nodes stay open. A node needs one iteration per dimension in its universe, the four canonical dimensions plus any custom name, and the depth-1 band gets only `budgetSplit.depth1` (0.5) of `--max-iterations`, so set `--max-iterations` to at least `2 * ceil(nodes / lineages) * dimensions` to fund the depth-1 band. It never outranks the iteration cap, stuck recovery, pause, `--convergence-mode=off` or `--stop-policy=max-iterations`. Fan-out lineages each get a share of the seeded nodes. |

.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/SKILL.md:238:- The flag writes `antiConvergence.depthMode` and the caps in `expansiveDepth`: `maxDepth` 3, `maxChildrenPerNode` 6 (the review root seed takes up to 8 depth-1 sub-targets, against 6 for research), a `0.5/0.35/0.15` budget split across the depth bands, and `maxFrontierNodes`, which defaults to half of `maxIterations` unless the config sets a number. `antiConvergence.depthMode` is locked against the optimizer; the `expansiveDepth` caps are in neither its tunable nor its locked list.
.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/SKILL.md:245:- A fan-out run deals the depth-1 nodes round-robin in node-id order, binds `frontier_scope:` beside `review_focus:`, records the partition as `frontier_partition` in `orchestration-summary.json` and returns a partition guard for any node no lineage reports. Size each lineage's iteration cap (the `--iters=N` value on its executor entry, which `fanout-run.cjs` passes as that lineage's `--max-iterations`, 12 when unset) so half of it covers `ceil(nodes / lineages) * dimensions`.
.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/README.md:158:`--depth-mode=expansive` is the one stop-path change that grows work instead of ending it. A seed step writes the depth-1 sub-target nodes before iteration 1, the active node's next uncovered dimension steers the iteration, and an admission step before the reducer applies six checks in order, relevance, dedupe, depth, fan-out, budget and cycle, pruning the proposal on the first failure. Node status lives in `review/frontier-nodes.jsonl`, which no default run creates, and an open node is filed as open search debt, so the run spends the iteration that node still needs. The frontier veto never outranks the iteration cap, consecutive failures, stuck recovery, pause, `--convergence-mode=off`, `--stop-policy=max-iterations` or the floor above, and stuck recovery closes the active node as `exhausted`. Once every node is closed and at least one was answered, a run that forms no other stop candidate gets `frontier_drained`, which the legal-stop gates still decide, so a run whose frontier work is done stops before its cap. The caps are `maxDepth` 3, `maxChildrenPerNode` 6, a `0.5/0.35/0.15` budget split and `maxFrontierNodes`.
.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/references/convergence/convergence.md:105:`--depth-mode=expansive` grows a depth-capped frontier of sub-target nodes beside the review target. The value lands in `antiConvergence.depthMode`, and the caps live in `expansiveDepth`: `maxDepth` 3, `maxChildrenPerNode` 6, a `budgetSplit` of 0.5/0.35/0.15 across the depth bands, and `maxFrontierNodes`, which defaults to half of `maxIterations` unless the config sets a number. The run cap never prunes a depth-1 seed, which only the root-children cap bounds, but every seed that is not pruned, answered ones included, counts toward the live total that later admissions are checked against. A fan-out parent seeds before any config exists, so its cap falls back to 10. The mode is locked against the optimizer; the `expansiveDepth` block is in neither its tunable nor its locked list. An absent flag means `default`, which runs exactly as before.
.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/references/state/state-format.md:155:| expansiveDepth | object | `{ maxDepth: 3, maxChildrenPerNode: 6, budgetSplit: {depth1: 0.5, depth2: 0.35, depth3: 0.15}, maxFrontierNodes }` | Caps for `--depth-mode=expansive`; `maxFrontierNodes` defaults to half of `maxIterations` unless the config sets a number |
.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/references/protocol/loop-protocol.md:459:Under `--depth-mode=expansive` the loop keeps a frontier of sub-target nodes beside the review target. The flag writes `antiConvergence.depthMode: "expansive"` and the caps in `expansiveDepth`: `maxDepth` 3, `maxChildrenPerNode` 6, a `budgetSplit` of 0.5/0.35/0.15 across the depth bands, and `maxFrontierNodes`, which defaults to half of `maxIterations` unless the config sets a number. A run without the flag keeps the default path and runs exactly as before, and the mode is locked against the optimizer; the `expansiveDepth` caps are in neither its tunable nor its locked list.
.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/README.md:127:`--depth-mode=expansive` lets a run grow its own questions. A seed step writes 3 to 6 depth-1 nodes before iteration 1, and the leaf may propose children only when its prompt carries a frontier block. An admission step before the reducer applies six checks in order, relevance, dedupe, depth, fan-out, budget and cycle, and prunes the proposal on the first failure. Next Focus reads the shallowest open node first and writes it as a frontier line, so visiting a node does not read as answering it. While a node stays open the frontier vetoes a soft convergence stop, which never outranks the iteration cap or the other hard stops, and the caps are `maxDepth` 3, `maxChildrenPerNode` 6, a `0.5/0.35/0.15` budget split and `maxFrontierNodes`.
.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/references/convergence/convergence.md:68:`--depth-mode=expansive` grows a frontier of follow-up questions beside the questions seeded at init. The value lands in `antiConvergence.depthMode`, and the caps live in `expansiveDepth`: `maxDepth` 3, `maxChildrenPerNode` 6, `budgetSplit` 0.5/0.35/0.15 for depths 1, 2 and 3, and `maxFrontierNodes`, which defaults to half of `maxIterations` unless the config sets a number. The run cap never prunes a depth-1 seed, which only the root-children cap bounds, but every seed that is not pruned, answered ones included, counts toward the live total that later admissions are checked against. A fan-out parent seeds before any config exists, so its cap falls back to 10. An absent flag means `default`, which keeps the default behavior; `research-depth-mode-golden.vitest` proves it after stripping the expansive-only persona paragraph from the CLI lineage prompt, the one byte change.

100:| `--depth-mode` | `default` \| `expansive` | `default`, the value when the flag is absent, runs exactly as before. `expansive` is opt-in: the run seeds depth-1 sub-target nodes from the review target before iteration 1 (merges are pruned as `merge-no-own-diff` and index rebuilds as `generated-artifact`), admits leaf `frontier_proposal` rows under depth, fan-out, budget, dedupe and cycle caps (`expansiveDepth` in the config: `maxDepth` 3, `maxChildrenPerNode` 6, a 50/35/15 iteration budget across depths), keeps node status in `review/frontier-nodes.jsonl`, holds open nodes as `searchDebt`, dispatches the active node's next uncovered dimension, and turns a review stop candidate into another iteration while nodes stay open. A node needs one iteration per dimension in its universe, the four canonical dimensions plus any custom name, and the depth-1 band gets only `budgetSplit.depth1` (0.5) of `--max-iterations`, so set `--max-iterations` to at least `2 * ceil(nodes / lineages) * dimensions` to fund the depth-1 band. It never outranks the iteration cap, stuck recovery, pause, `--convergence-mode=off` or `--stop-policy=max-iterations`. Fan-out lineages each get a share of the seeded nodes. |

[0m$ [0mcd /Users/michelkerkmeester/worktrees/public/455-expansive-depth-integration && sed -n '1210,1245p' .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/scripts/reduce-state.cjs && echo "=== 1590-1615 convergence" && sed -n '1590,1615p' .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/scripts/convergence.cjs
return { openFindings, resolvedFindings };

function compareFindings(left, right) {
  const severityOrder = { P0: 0, P1: 1, P2: 2 };
  const severityDiff = (severityOrder[left.severity] ?? 9) - (severityOrder[right.severity] ?? 9);
  if (severityDiff !== 0) {
    return severityDiff;
  }
  return left.findingId.localeCompare(right.findingId);
}

// The canonical four come first in risk order; configured custom names follow in configured order.
function buildDimensionUniverse(config) {
  const universe = [...REQUIRED_DIMENSIONS];
  const raw = config && config.reviewDimensions;
  const configured = Array.isArray(raw) ? raw : (typeof raw === 'string' ? raw.split(',') : []);
  for (const entry of configured) {
    const name = String(entry).trim().toLowerCase();
    if (name && name !== 'all' && !universe.includes(name)) {
      universe.push(name);
    }
  }
  return universe;
}

function deriveDimension(finding, iteration, universe = REQUIRED_DIMENSIONS) {
  const focus = `${iteration.focus} ${finding.title} ${finding.description}`.toLowerCase();
  for (const dimension of universe) {
    if (focus.includes(dimension)) {
      return dimension;
    }
  }
  return iteration.dimensionsAddressed[0] || 'correctness';
}

  const dimensions = Array.isArray(config.reviewDimensions)
    ? config.reviewDimensions
    : Object.keys(coverage);
  const findingsBySeverity = isPlainObject(registry.findingsBySeverity)
    ? registry.findingsBySeverity
    : {};
  const openFindings = Array.isArray(registry.openFindings) ? registry.openFindings : [];
  const searchCoverage = isPlainObject(registry.searchCoverage) ? registry.searchCoverage : {};
  const candidateCoverage = isPlainObject(registry.candidateCoverage) ? registry.candidateCoverage : {};
  const lastIteration = iterationRecords.length > 0
    ? iterationRecords[iterationRecords.length - 1]
    : null;

  return {
    totalDimensions: dimensions.length,
    missingDimensions: dimensions.filter((dimension) => coverage[dimension] !== true),
    coverageAge: reviewCoverageAge(evidenceRecords, dimensions),

.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/feature-catalog/convergence/divergent-convergence-mode.md:22:The workflow admits the exact reasons `composite_converged` and `all_questions_answered`. A successful pivot selects a new focus from persisted adjacent questions, contradiction or verification gaps, missing source classes, alternate evidence methods, and independent finding checks. The shared adapter runs three native seats in one round. A first-attempt quorum miss gets a one-time quorum retry of the same three seats, so one pivot uses at most six seat outputs. The adapter persists the transaction under `<artifactRoot>/divergent/pivots/<pivotId>/council/**`, restores the selected focus, and continues the loop.

.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/references/protocol/loop-protocol.md:411:Under `--depth-mode=expansive` the loop keeps a frontier of follow-up questions beside the key questions seeded at init. The flag writes `antiConvergence.depthMode: "expansive"` and the caps in `expansiveDepth`: `maxDepth` 3, `maxChildrenPerNode` 6, a `budgetSplit` of 0.5/0.35/0.15 per depth, and `maxFrontierNodes`, which defaults to half of `maxIterations` unless the config sets a number. A run without the flag keeps the default path and its behavior. The one byte change is the expansive-only persona paragraph in the CLI lineage prompt, which the golden test strips before it compares.
.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/references/protocol/loop-protocol.md:413:`step_frontier_seed` decomposes the topic into 3 to 6 depth-1 nodes before iteration 1. On a plain run it sits in `phase_init` after `step_create_config`; on a fan-out run it runs before `step_fanout_spawn`. It calls `runtime/scripts/frontier-admit.cjs seed`.
.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/references/protocol/loop-protocol.md:415:A leaf may append `frontier_proposal` rows to its delta only when its prompt carries a FRONTIER block, which `frontier-admit.cjs block` computes from the node's remaining child slots and the prompt pack renders through `{frontier_block}`. `step_frontier_admit` runs before `step_reduce_state` and calls `frontier-admit.cjs admit`, which applies six checks in order: relevance, dedupe, depth, fan-out, budget, cycle. The first failure prunes the proposal with that reason. Admitted nodes become `research/inbox.jsonl` rows with `origin: "frontier"`.
.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/references/protocol/loop-protocol.md:419:Under stuck recovery the handler closes the focused node as `exhausted` with reason `stuck` through `frontier-admit.cjs close`, keeps the `stuckRecovery` event, and moves to the next open node. Global recovery runs only when no node is open.
.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/references/protocol/loop-protocol.md:421:On a fan-out run, `fanout-run.cjs --depth-mode expansive` deals the seeded open depth-1 nodes round-robin in node-id order across lineages, writes each lineage's `frontier-nodes.jsonl` and inbox rows before it spawns, binds `frontier_scope:` in the lineage prompt and records `frontier_partition` in `orchestration-summary.json`. `fanout-merge.cjs` merges the lineage frontiers, each node keeping its lineage label, and records a partition guard that names any dealt node no lineage reports.

.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/changelog/v1.3.1.0.md:40:When an operator re-entered a review session, the interactive workflow offered `resume` (continue where you left off) and `restart`. It also offered `fork` (start a sibling run history). It offered `completed-continue` as well (reopen a finished run after snapshotting its synthesis). Only `resume` and `restart` were implemented. Selecting either unsupported mode did not branch the run. The state log silently kept the same session ID. That left earlier work open to overwrite. `fork` and `completed-continue` are now explicitly deferred. The skill guidance, workflows and runtime mirrors all mark both modes as deferred. The gate offers `resume`, `restart` or `cancel`. `restart` starts a new generation. Both paths append a typed event to the state log. That event carries the run history fields. They are session ID, parent session ID, run history mode, continued-from-run, generation, archive path and timestamp.

Configuration, JSONL, strategy and command docs described consistent run-history fields for all four. The v1.5.0.0 upgrade note said `resume` and `completed-continue` carried the v1.4.0.0 lineage contract. That contract was extended with legal-stop gate results and optional graph events.
